## B002 | 2022-11-04 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Starting off with something that I always wanted to talk about, freelancing, I’ve done it through the platform Fiverr - reasons the platform worked for me:

- the buyers approach you instead of you  looking and chasing after potential clients
- “a gig” can be setup easily in 15 mins, which is the only thing that you need to get started
- the buyers pay in advance the full amount to Fiverr which is on hold until you deliver the service, hence the payment system is secure

Overall, start freelancing, whichever platform suits you and your style of work.

#fiverr #freelancing

## B018 | 2022-12-01 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
How to price yourself as a freelancer, from someone with almost a year of experience in the field:

- Investment the client is willing to make: the person who quotes first loses, try finding out how much the client values your service before you quote

- Finding the market rate: research and talk to people in your industry, and set your rates around the same price

- Hours you put in: roughly calculate the amount of time the work will take you and a fair hourly rate you think you’d like to work in

Trust your gut when it comes to pricing and don’t undervalue yourself, start out at less but raise rates with every experience.

#freelancing #pricing

## B043 | 2023-05-24 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
As a 19 year old entrepreneur with an experience of over a year of being in the field of marketing, I have grown and learned in countless personal and professional areas. Here are my 3 key lessons:

👩‍💻 Work and Passion
I absolutely love what I do, but there are times when this turns into a love-hate relationship. I’ve read 12+ books encouraging me to “follow my passion” or “work won’t feel like work if you love what you do”, but the hard reality being sometimes it does.

I’ve overburdened myself with the guilt for not romanticizing my work everyday or for ending the day with completing only the must - haves. But I’ve learnt to understand that going to college every day for 8 hours is not nothing, maintaining a clientele of 8 brands is not just something small and having a team of 4 is also not easy. Sure, you love what you do but sometimes being passionate is not enough, which brings me to my next learning.

⏰ Routine and Discipline
I dislike my days looking the same and hate having the conventional notion of a time table like routine. I thrive when there’s something new to do in a day, be it as easy as waking up early to make breakfast. Some days, I need to be around loud music and people. The realization and acceptance has made me have my own system of productivity and accountability, taking bits and pieces from books on productivity and studying the routine of people in general.

Coming to discipline, what has worked for me is looking at the bigger picture, envisioning my goals, and imagining the results. I learned the importance of balancing work, college and relationships in amounts that feel right to me. I remember I earlier used to close off to work constantly feeling like nothing else is as important but that led to burnout.

👫 Client and Team Management
You can’t make everyone happy. On countless occasions, I have put myself through underpaying or rude or overburdening clients just because I felt like I’m just starting out or am somehow inferior. Letting go of that belief, took a little more time than it should have but I can happily say I got out of such a situation in my own time.

Circling back to my system, every client and every team member requires an approach that seems most comfortable to them, even being the littlest things like some clients considering conversations smoother over weekly or if over daily meetings. Understanding the psychology and likings of the person, be it a client or a team member is something that makes my company’s management as unique and people centric as it could be.

In summary, as a 19-year-old entrepreneur, I've learned to balance passion with realistic expectations, embrace routine and discipline, and prioritize understanding and catering to the needs of clients and team members. I hope sharing these lessons benefits you and provides you an insight into the entrepreneurial journey.

Looking forward to hearing your thoughts and learnings in the comments! ❤️

## B044 | 2023-06-02 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I don’t have a fixed time table or schedule for how things go about in my daily life and yes I understand the importance of it. So, what I do instead is block time in my calendar which has skyrocketed my productivity 🚀

My mom taught me to study for a designated period of time even if it means I switch subjects in between, which helped my younger self beat the boredom. Time blocking involves you to designate a set amount of hours to a broad task, this helps you stay disciplined, provide you with a sense of deadline and beat your procrastination.

For eg: I set client work from 11 am to 1 pm - this gives me time to focus on one aspect of my business for a set amount of time during the day. The client work could range from designing graphics to curating brand proposals, always keeping it a mix of mini tasks 🕣

It is still a somewhat structured schedule and has helped me achieve a healthy work - life balance. Comment down below your productivity tips!

#productivity #timeblocking

## B045 | 2023-06-05 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
2 years ago, I was this shiny kid attending college orientation led by this senior, she was heading the club that everyone wanted to be a part of, that looked extremely fun and I was in instant awe, there was something magical in how she was doing it.

I thought “This is where I want to be” as someone who was afraid of putting herself out there but to be where the senior was, THAT looked like something to gather the courage for. And I did. I was a step away from being that and it suddenly felt worth so little.

I was exactly where I wanted to be, but I, 2 years later, thought there were bigger opportunities worth pursuing. Every single day, I dream bigger because I fully believe in myself to reach there, as this is what I was able to learn:

🎤 I fell in love with being on stage

I started public speaking at each and every event. I had a full blown panic attack my second time, but I got on stage and I’ve never wanted to get down since. I was trained in public speaking in primary school but ah it had been long.

Being an active participant of the club led me to realize what I want to do my whole life.

👭 I fell in love with working with people

Collaborating, discussing and delegating became a part of every day. Every person was an opportunity to learn from and I absorbed as much as I could.

I learned the power of opinions, the power of speaking up and the power of consistently working hard alongside others.

🫶🏻 I fell in love with leadership

I always knew I liked leading, but the extent of which became clearer as I started to. I lead a team of 4 employees now, and that wouldn’t have been possible if I didn’t start off with leading my peers.

Most of all, I fell in love with my abilities. I get chills every time I think I own a business now, I ACTUALLY am living the bigger dream. That club, that senior, and that wave of inspiration - it made me exploit my potential in more ways than I can count.

I can’t wait to learn and pursue more. Now that I have the courage, I am building in public - join my newsletter to get in on the journey.

#business #leadership

## B048 | 2023-06-22 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
As a marketing agency owner, the way brands are formed and the thought process behind each action taken by a brand is intriguing. So, I attended a workshop on brand dissection conducted by Pranita Bajoria. Here are 3 ways it has been helping my business:

🪄 Effective Strategy Planning

By dissecting multiple brands and analyzing the content their audience is engaging with, I am now able to build effective content strategies that keep my clients on par with the industry level.

👀 Competitor Analysis

I have started conducting a comprehensive analysis of the brand's competitors in the social media landscape. By examining their social media presence, content strategy, engagement tactics, and audience interactions - I am able to identify gaps and opportunities that can differentiate my client's brand and give it a competitive advantage.

✨ Overcoming Imposter Syndrome

Attending a workshop with 40 other professionals in the space helped me feel like I'm not alone, and helped me in finding more people I can connect with to geek out over all things social media.

I believe continuous learning and networking are important skills. This is what I learned from dedicating 2 hours to my personal growth, and I am here to learn and share more with you.

#marketing #socialmedia

## B061 | 2023-08-07 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I curated content for a jewelry brand, a makeup artist, and a fashion label while being on an editorial shoot for my client, and here are the 3️⃣ things I learned:

👑 Content is king!

Whether you are just selling plants or you have an entire luxury clothing line, whichever industry you lie in - you NEED to have content on your socials. Neglecting your social media is making you lose lakhs in business!

Every single team member at a shoot of 8 was focused on shooting content for their social media because that is what truly drives business - your organic and authentic self.

🤝🏻 Power of Networking

Well-established businesses with over 5-7 years of experience rely on word of mouth as a crucial channel to grow their business. As important as social media is, networking and spreading the word about your business in every place you go to cannot be neglected.

Network. Network. Network. You never know what expertise you need when. I exchanged business cards and conversations with the entire team to both provide an insight into what I do and also to learn from what they do.

❤️ Strong Team & Motivation
As much as money is a motivating factor, having people who are truly passionate and love what they do is what makes it or breaks it. Find people who understand your vision!

The shoot wouldn't have been a success unless all 8 of us were dedicated to bringing the best out of each other and the resources available. Whatever you choose to do, pour your heart into it.

- What next?
→ Follow me

## B062 | 2023-08-15 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I have decided to make 3 HUGE lifestyle changes! 🫢

As you know, change is always daunting and difficult, but the wisdom from the 20+ books I've read on productivity shines during these times ☀️

Change 1️⃣: Eating Healthier
If you were to look at me, you'd never believe the amount of junk I eat throughout the day - the downhill journey started with just one packet of chips and escalated to ordering out once a day! 🍟

Now, building habits is tough. I'm starting by having a protein-packed breakfast and dinner, and giving myself free rein for lunch for the first 2 weeks. Recognizing that taking smaller steps leads to greater progress rather than going all-in and burning out is what I'll swear by on this journey 🥓

Change 2️⃣: Moving My Body
The majority of my day is spent working on the laptop, which means sitting on a chair for extended periods of time without any movement 👩‍💻

I've decided to walk around the house more and shift most of my work to my phone, which will provide me with more flexibility. Apart from this, I love pilates, and a 10-minute home workout seems easily achievable 🧘‍♀️

Change 3️⃣: Being More Creative
I succumbed to the hustle culture, and my work became the only outlet for my creativity. I enjoyed reading and managed to read 30 books in a year until I started my own business. Every time I felt like doing something, I'd resort to working 😩

I realized how limiting this became for my personal development and have started to rebuild my reading habit from scratch. Reading 30 pages a day is a small step that will rekindle my love for the activity 📚

- What's Next?
→ Follow me to join in on my journey!

## B063 | 2023-09-15 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
3️⃣ reasons I regret not doing more internships 👩‍💻:

1️⃣ Exposure - Working in a company that share the same interests as you provides you with exposure into the industry 👭

You get to meet people with the same motives that provide you support in the long run. You network 🤝🏻

I would love to have the chance to be surrounded by people who love social media as much as I do, who manage clients every day and are in leadership positions, an internship (among other things) could have possibly given me that.

2️⃣ Understanding operations - Being in a MNC provides you with the opportunity to have an in depth understanding of the inner workings of running the business 👀

When you’re starting out, something as basic as having an optimum hierarchical structure is necessary which I believe I would have learnt faster after interning in multiple companies 🧳

3️⃣ Experiences - there are SO many people to talk to; even if you talk to 1 new person every week - you have an opportunity to learn about their experiences of working in a corporate, the challenges they face and why they do what they do.

You learn from the experiences of others while also experiencing some part of the world yourself 🌏

## B067 | 2023-09-17 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I believe that when I say things, I can make them happen 💪🏻

I believe that every thought in my head is answered for - and that's manifestation.

Here are 3️⃣ ways to manifest your dream life:

1️⃣ Vision Board - I love the idea of having all your dreams and goals right in front of you, every day 🏆

Go to Pinterest and be really specific about what you want. Want to start eating healthy? Print out a picture of a chia seed bowl and a green smoothie and place it on there. It’s time for you to be crafty! ✂️

2️⃣ Positive thinking - It took me a while, but every time I have a negative thought in my head, I consciously replace it with a positive one.

You can’t put out things you don’t want out in the universe. You have to start tricking your brain into thinking nothing but positivity☀️

3️⃣ Visualisation - Close your eyes and think about you achieving your goal. No, really, think about every single detail while placing yourself in that scenario.

I prefer to visualize what my next day looks like every night before I go to sleep, but you can start with it anytime and anywhere  👀

Manifestation really isn’t as complicated as advertised. You need to start believing in yourself and in the power of the universe.

Which technique would you try? 💭

#manifestation #positivethinking #dreamlife #goals

Your FREE actionable guide to starting your OWN social media management agency 💸

Step 1: What is SMMA? What services do you start with? 🤔

💬 So, you’ve decided you want to start your own SMMA—congrats! But what is it exactly?

👩‍💻 A social media management agency helps businesses maintain an online presence on various social media platforms like Instagram, Facebook, etc.

Now that you have a gist of what an SMMA is, let’s dive right into the actionable steps!

Here is your first consideration: What services does your SMMA offer? 🫶🏻

Some services offered as the norm in the industry are:

1️⃣Strategy Development: Creating plans to achieve social media goals and objectives.
2️⃣Content Creation: Producing engaging posts, images, and videos.
3️⃣Posting and Scheduling: Timely sharing of content on social platforms.
4️⃣Community Engagement: Interacting with followers, responding to comments, and messages.
5️⃣Analytics and Reporting: Monitoring performance and providing insights.

I was sick for 3 days, which meant a task list longer than I could tackle 🤒

After feeling EXTREMELY overwhelmed,

🤗 "Break" Tasks

🚶‍♀️Tasks I could do while walking

Walking around gives me a dopamine boost, so all tasks that could be done on my phone while walking fell into this category.

## B206 | 2024-05-03 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I recently got rid of a deadweight client 🥶

It took me 6 months to execute this decision and I’m so proud of myself that I did.

Why did I make this decision?

⛳️ She didn’t acknowledge invoices and payment reminders - only for it to get extended by 20 to 30 days

⛳️ She had the attitude that I work “for” her and not “with” her and that showed up in MANY uncomfortable scenarios

⛳️ She used to increase the deliverables at her convenience and wanted to get as much work done as possible as “small favours” or “every other social media agency includes it” in order to not pay me or my team for our time and effort

Why did it take me 6 months to get out of this?

💰The delay in payments? that always meant that I wasn’t paid for my team’s work and felt like I shouldn’t get out without getting paid

## B249 | 2024-08-08 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Can’t believe I am writing this,

21 things I learned at 21.

21. Nobody knows what they’re doing
– There is no set roadmap, no set guidebook and no instructions to follow

20. Hype up yourself
— Because nobody else will, you’re your own cheerleader

19. Your mindset is everything
— You have the ability to radiate at the highest frequency

18. Your thoughts hold power
— Everything you think & wish for will eventually come true

17. Not being hard on yourself
— Treat yourself with kindness

16. Know your worth
— Don’t let anyone ever dictate your worth

15. Learn to love spending time with yourself
— You’re your own best friend

14. Your goals first
— Don’t put anything or anyone above your goals & aspirations

13. Your opinion overpowers any one else’s
— Do what you think is right & stand for your opinions

12. Be grateful
— Everything that you are is a blessing & never take that for granted

11. You create your own path
— What works for others will not 100% ever work for you

10. Find solutions, not problems
— Have a solution-oriented mindset & know that you can solve everything

9. It’s okay to take a pause
— You’re not a robot or a machine, take a minute for yourself

8. There’s always time
— Make time for things that matter to you; time management is a skill that you learn

7. Your imperfections are your strengths
— You have the power to feel secure & turn it into strengths

6. You don’t need to fit in
— You will eventually find your people, don’t change yourself

5. Be more patient with yourself
— If you aren’t able to do it today, doesn’t mean that you will never be able to

4. Power of compounding
— All your small actions & habits compound to huge changes with time

3. Your time is valuable
— Don’t waste your time in activities that wouldn’t yield you happiness or result one year down the line

2. Listen more, talk less
— there is something to learn from everyone you meet so listen

1. Money matters in life
— Money gives you the power to be free

P.S. If you read till the end, comment “🧿” to let me know. Grateful for you.

## B255 | 2024-08-17 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
What is UGC & should you invest in it?

Welcome to day 5/15 of my weekly series - Small Business Saturdays, where we discuss what you can do to market your business better.

UGC marketing i.e. user-generated content is all the rage right now!

But what does it do for your brand?

1. Increased Authenticity and Trust
— UGC often feels more genuine and trustworthy than brand-generated content. Potential customers are more likely to trust peer reviews and real-life usage stories than traditional advertising.

2. Cost-Effective Content Creation
— Leveraging UGC can significantly reduce content creation costs. Instead of investing heavily in creating new content, brands can utilise photos, videos, reviews, and other content produced by users.

3. Broadened Reach and Organic Promotion
—- When users share content related to a brand, it extends the brand's reach to their networks, often leading to organic promotion.

Do you think it’s right for you?
Comment down below your questions & I’ll resolve them for you! 👇🏻

#UGC #socialmediamanagement #smallbusinesses

## B297 | 2024-11-25 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
5 days, 5 HOT questions 🔥
Q5 - What truly converts on social media?

If you want to drive real results on social media, here’s what actually converts:

1️⃣ Authentic Content
People can spot a sales pitch from a mile away. What works? Real, relatable content that builds trust and connects with your audience.

2️⃣ Clear Calls to Action (CTAs)
It sounds simple, but clear, direct CTAs are key to conversions.

Example: Myntra uses clear and catchy CTAs like “Shop Now” and “Get the Look,” making it easy for followers to click through and make purchases instantly.

3️⃣ User-Generated Content (UGC)
When your audience shares their experiences with your product or service, it’s the most powerful social proof.

Example: Mamaearth encourages customers to share their experiences with skincare products, using hashtags like #MamaearthGlow.

💡 The key to converting? Be genuine, clear, and consistent in your approach.

What’s been your most successful conversion tactic on social media? Let me know in the comments! 👇🏻

#socialmediamanagement #socialmediamarketing #socialmedia

## B298 | 2024-11-25 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
5 days 5 HOT questions 🔥
Q4 - Should I hire in-house or have an agency?

If you’re serious about fast growth and expert execution, here’s why I’d recommend an agency:

1️⃣ Expertise On-Demand
Agencies bring years of experience and specialized skills, giving you instant access to top-tier professionals. No need to spend months recruiting or training.

2️⃣ Fresh Ideas: They offer an outsider's perspective and creative input that can push your brand in new, exciting directions.

3️⃣ Scalability: Need more content for a launch or a campaign? An agency can scale up quickly without the overhead costs of hiring more staff.

4️⃣ Results-Driven: Agencies are focused on one thing - getting results. They’re constantly optimizing to give you the best ROI possible.

5️⃣ Cost-Effective: Hiring a full in-house team can be expensive, agency fees often provide a more affordable way to get high-quality work across multiple areas (content, ads, strategy, etc.) without a full-time salary commitment.

In-House might sound like a good idea if you want total control, but it often comes with slow execution, higher costs, and limited expertise.

💡 The takeaway? If you want to move fast, stay ahead of trends, and get expert results, an agency is the smart choice.

What’s your approach? In-house or agency? Let me know in the comments! 👇🏻

#socialmediamanagement #socialmedia #socialmediamarketing

media?

Example: boAt Lifestyle has a bold, energetic aesthetic that appeals to India’s young, tech-savvy crowd. Their social media screams lifestyle, not just earphones.

Example: Dunzo is a master of riding trends and connecting with the pulse of what’s happening, whether it’s cricket, Bollywood, or quirky day-to-day moments.

## B300 | 2024-11-25 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
5 days 5 HOT questions 🔥
Q2 - Will social media get me sales?

The short answer? Yes, but it works differently depending on your business. Here's how:

For D2C Brands:
1️⃣ Social media builds awareness and trust; people buy from brands they recognize.
2️⃣ Platforms like Instagram and YouTube Shorts drive impulse purchases with the right visuals, trends, and offers.
3️⃣ Paid ads help you target exactly who’s ready to buy.

For B2B Brands:
1️⃣ It’s about relationships, not just transactions; social media nurtures trust over time.
2️⃣ LinkedIn and Twitter are great for positioning your expertise and building connections.
3️⃣ Social media warms up leads so your sales team can close deals faster.

💡 The key is to play the long game: build credibility, engage consistently, and sales will follow.

What’s your business type? Comment down below 👇🏻

#socialmedia #socialmediamanagement #socialmediamarketing

## B301 | 2024-11-25 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
5 days 5 HOT questions 🔥
Q1 - When is the right time to invest in social media?

I’ve worked with over 30 brands and helped founders leverage social media at various stages of their business.

Here's when I believe it’s non-negotiable to invest:

1️⃣ For a Big Launch
Launching something new? Social media is how you grab attention.

Example: We helped a women’s western wear brand create such a buzz that their pre-launch content got over 203 shares on Day 1.

2️⃣ When Growth Hits a Wall If sales slow or engagement drops, social media can reignite momentum.

3️⃣ To Expand an Offline Success Already thriving offline? Social media can multiply your reach.
Example: For a 20-year-old menswear brand, we used celebrity spottings and influencer collabs to transform their legacy into digital growth.

💡 The right time to invest in social media? When you’re ready to scale, connect, and grow.

Here are the top 3 mistakes I made running my social media agency:

1️⃣ Leniency
Not having strict policies on absences, timings, and delays.

2️⃣ Add-on Deliverables
Offering extra deliverables or services at no cost to clients.

3️⃣ Pressure

## B325 | 2025-01-05 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
My 7 professional learnings of 2024:

1️⃣ Clarity attracts success
Being clear about our niche and services helped us target the right clients and stand out in a competitive market.

2️⃣ I had to let go of control
Delegating was tough at first, but watching my team step up and deliver showed me that trust is the best leadership tool.

3️⃣ Not every day feels like a win
Some months were harder than others, and I realized that growth isn’t always linear.

4️⃣ Burnout is real
I thought I could do it all until I couldn’t. Setting boundaries, taking breaks, and leaning on my team helped me regain balance.

5️⃣ South Ex is more than a location
Having an office here helped me feel like I belonged in the world of business. It wasn’t just about credibility; it gave me confidence.

6️⃣ Client trust is everything
The best clients this year weren’t just paying us; they believed in us. That trust made me want to overdeliver every single time.

7️⃣ I’m just getting started
2024 taught me that the journey is long, but every single step matters. The wins, the struggles, the lessons - they’re all shaping the entrepreneur I’m becoming.

## B328 | 2025-01-05 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Does your personal brand pass the visibility test?

Let’s be real - if people can’t find you or don’t know what you’re about, how will opportunities come your way?

Your personal brand is your story, and it’s what helps you stand out.

Here’s a 5-step checklist to see where you stand:

1️⃣ Can people find you online?
Google your name. What comes up to show your achievements and expertise, or is it blank?

2️⃣ Are you active on LinkedIn?
Posting once in a while won’t help. You need to show up regularly to stay on people’s radar.

3️⃣ Does your content tell your story?
Your posts should clearly share what you do, what you believe in, and why it matters. If people don’t “get” you, they won’t remember you.

4️⃣ Are you connecting with the right people?
Your network should include people who align with your goals - clients, partners, or even mentors.

5️⃣ Do opportunities come to you?
When your personal brand is strong, people start reaching out to you for collaborations, interviews, or speaking gigs.

If you’re not ticking all these boxes, it’s time to make a change. Your personal brand should work for you - not the other way around.

Brands love to say influencer marketing doesn’t work.

“These influencers don’t drive sales.”

Influencer marketing isn’t the problem; your approach is.

1️⃣ You’re Paying for Eyeballs, Not Sales

• Influencers build trust, not instant conversions.
• Expecting them to replace performance ads is a rookie mistake.
• They generate attention - you need to nurture and convert.

2️⃣ You’re Choosing Influencers Wrong
• A 10K-follower creator with real influence converts better than a 500K-follower model with random brand deals.
• Engagement > Follower count. Pick influencers whose audience actually listens.

3️⃣ You Have No Follow-Up Strategy
• An influencer post alone won’t make people buy. Retarget, email, and nurture.
• Smart brands turn influencer-driven traffic into repeat customers.

The bottom line?
Influencer marketing works only if you use it right.

## B360 | 2025-02-25 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I have lost over ₹1,00,000 in performance marketing
and here are my 3️⃣ biggest takeaways:

1. Targeting
Yes, a broad target audience will get you more leads, but niche targeting is what will actually get your ICPs.

Don't get caught up in vanity metrics like impressions or clicks; it's about conversion at the end of the day.

2. Testing and Optimisation
If you’re not constantly testing, you’re missing out. Performance marketing is all about iterative improvement.

From creatives and copy to landing pages and bidding strategies, A/B testing is crucial to find what truly works.

3. Tracking and Analytics
I cannot stress this enough: without proper tracking, you're flying blind.

Setting up robust tracking, whether through UTM parameters, pixels, or third-party tools, is essential for knowing what’s working and what’s not.

Losing money is never fun, but these lessons have made me a more strategic, data-driven marketer.

What truly makes or breaks your LinkedIn growth? Engagement!

Engagement has helped increase my clients' impressions by 4x.

Research, research, research. Spend time reading the comment sections of creators and identify comments that spark engagement. Mimic those to build your own authentic voice.

AI Marketing Tools Review
Part 1: Marky

Tried planning content but felt stuck?
Marky is an AI tool that helps with content strategy, planning, and ideas so you don’t have to start from scratch.

Who is it for?

• Business owners who need content ideas but don’t have time.
• Creators & marketers who want help with content calendars & trends.
• Startups looking for structured marketing plans.

What does it do well?

• Gives ready-to-use content plans – Just enter a topic, and it suggests hooks, formats, and timelines.
• Finds trending topics – It analyzes what’s working and recommends ideas.
• Saves time – Cuts brainstorming time by half.

Where does it struggle?

• Ideas can feel generic – AI still lacks human creativity.
• Limited customization – Needs manual tweaks to match your brand’s voice.
• Not a full marketing solution – It plans, but you still have to execute.

Final Verdict: 4/5
A great tool for content planning & marketing strategy, but you’ll still need a personal touch.

## B374 | 2025-03-18 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
“I don’t have time for content.”

That’s what a C-suite executive told me last month. Between meetings, travel, and decision-making, content always took a backseat.

So, I gave her a 5-minute content strategy. Here’s how it works:

1. Capture (1 min)
After a call, meeting, or event, jot down one key insight you shared.
This is your raw content.

2. Simplify (2 min)
Turn that insight into a short LinkedIn post:
What happened? (Set the scene)
What’s the lesson? (Make it clear)
Why does it matter? (Tie it to business)

3. Post & Engage (2 min)
Hit publish and reply to 2-3 comments throughout the day.

That’s it. No overthinking. No overcomplicating. Just real thoughts, shared simply.

Your audience doesn’t need perfection.
They need your insights.

Here’s your easy 3-step guide.

Use this prompt (customize it with your details):

Create a photorealistic action figure of the person in the photo.

The figure should be full-body and placed inside a clear plastic box with a colorful cardboard background, just like real collectible toys.

Make the packaging look as realistic as possible - shiny plastic, a top hook for hanging, and toy-store-style design.

Place accessories next to the figure that reflect its style and image: [list accessories]

On the box:
-   At the top, write in large letters: [your name]
-   Below that - description: [your text]

Make the image as realistic as possible as if it's a real toy you'd find in a store.

Get creative!

## B402 | 2025-04-24 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Everyone’s suddenly obsessed with EGC.
But is it just UGC with a new name or actually better for your brand?

EGC = Employee Generated Content
UGC = User Generated Content
Both = Gold when done right

EGC shows what happens inside your company:
- Culture
- Behind-the-scenes
- Team voices & values

It’s perfect for employer branding, recruiting, and thought leadership.

UGC shows how your audience experiences you:
- Reviews
- Testimonials
- Unboxings
- Real product usage

It’s magic for D2C, consumer trust, and conversions.

But here’s the real test:
- Are your employees excited enough to rep your brand online?
- Are your customers talking about you without being paid?

If not - neither strategy will work.

So, which one’s right for your brand?
EGC or UGC? Or do you need both? Comment down below 👇🏻

## B407 | 2025-05-17 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Is ghostwriting inauthentic?
Not at all.
It’s a tool for founders who want to show up consistently without the pressure.

Here’s how it works and why it’s effective:

1. Capture your voice
A good ghostwriter listens to you. They work with you to understand your tone, style, and ideas.
The goal? To make sure your personality shines through every post.

2. Save time
As a busy CEO, you don’t have hours to write. By partnering with a ghostwriter, you free up time to focus on your business while still building your personal brand.

3. Stay consistent
Visibility is key. You need to post regularly to be seen, but the pressure of writing daily can cause burnout.
With ghostwriting, you can stay consistent without sacrificing quality.

3️⃣ Quick Takeaways:

- If you’re struggling with time or consistency, consider ghostwriting.
- Focus on key topics and themes, then let the writer turn them into posts.
- Aim for at least 3-4 posts a week to stay top-of-mind.

You don’t have to do it alone and you don’t have to write every word yourself to be authentic.

## B450 | 2025-11-09 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Good writing isn’t about adding more.
It’s about removing what doesn’t matter.

Here’s my 10-minute edit rule that 2x my clients post performance:

1. Cut 20% of word
Example:
❌ “In today’s world, creating content is very important for building your brand.”
✅ “Creating content builds your brand.”

2. Shorten long sentences
❌ “This helped me realize that consistency is the key factor in long-term growth.”
✅ “This made me realize that consistency wins.”

3. Delete fillers like “that,” “really,” “actually.”
❌ “I really think that it’s actually important to simplify.”
✅ “It’s important to simplify.”

Writing is thinking made visible.
Editing is clarity made powerful.

If you spend 30 minutes writing,
spend 10 tightening.
That’s the difference between good and great content.

## B476 | 2026-01-05 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
7 LinkedIn hacks that are so good they feel illegal.

1️⃣ Post at 7:13 AM (not 7:00 AM) - beats the algorithm cluster

2️⃣ Edit your post 3 minutes after publishing to trigger a second algorithm push

3️⃣ Tag 1 person in comments (not the post) to restart engagement 2 hours later

4️⃣ Steal your best-performing tweet and repost it on LinkedIn word-for-word

5️⃣ Comment on 10 posts in your feed before you publish yours (algorithm prioritizes active users)

6️⃣ Use a period on its own line to create artificial white space (breaks up text walls)

7️⃣ Screenshot your post, post it as an image, put the text in comments (doubles reach)

Most founders play fair with the algorithm.
Smart founders play the game.

## B514 | 2026-04-29 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Your post doesn’t die at the 4-hour mark.
It dies at the 48-hour mark.

And most leaders are asleep at the wheel for both.

Here’s what’s actually happening after you hit publish:

→ Hour 0 to 1: LinkedIn shows your post to a tiny test audience. Engagement here decides everything.
→ Hour 1 to 4: If the test worked, reach expands. Comments pull harder than likes.
→ Hour 4 to 24: 80% of leaders disappear. Check impressions once, close the app.
→ Hour 24 to 48: Post is still being served. Every new comment restarts momentum.
→ Hour 48+: Post is done. The people it attracted are still watching.

Most leaders treat a post like a broadcast. Hit publish, walk away.

The reason isn’t laziness. It’s time.

Engagement done properly is 2 to 3 hours a day. That’s the part leaders can’t outsource because it has to sound like them.

## B519 | 2026-05-15 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Likes build audiences. DMs build businesses.

You'll always optimize for what you measure. And most leaders on LinkedIn are measuring the wrong things.

Six shifts that change what LinkedIn does for you:

• "Viral post" → "Specific post"
A viral post brings a thousand strangers to your profile. A specific post brings the one buyer who's been waiting.

• "Engagement" → "Inbound"
Engagement is activity. Inbound is intent. One looks busy. The other writes checks.

• "Follower count" → "DM count"
50K followers and zero DMs is failure dressed as success. 5K followers and five calls a week is the business.

• "Posting" → "Briefing"
A bad brief makes a bad post nothing can save. The work is upstream of the keyboard.

• "Likes" → "Saves"
Likes are dopamine. Saves are intent. The algorithm watches saves. Your buyer watches them too.

• "Going viral" → "Getting cited"
Viral fades in 48 hours. Citation compounds. Buyers remember the post that named the thing they're trying to name.

You build for what you measure. Measure what closes.

11 signs your LinkedIn is leaking pipeline.

You're posting. You're getting some likes.

But the calls aren't coming.

Here's what's quietly costing you clients:

1. Your DMs have no path
↳ A warm lead shows interest and you wing it, so they go cold.

2. You're measuring the wrong thing
↳ Likes feel good, but conversations are what actually pay you.

3. Your featured section is empty
↳ The prime real estate that could book you sits unused.

4. You post and then disappear
↳ No replies in the first hour, so the post never travels.

5. You share opinions but never proof
↳ Without results and screenshots, trust never gets built.

6. There's no clear next step for anyone interested
↳ They want in, but you've given them nowhere to go.

7. Your headline says your title, not your value
↳ "Founder" tells me nothing about who you help or how.

8. You only ever show up in your own comments
↳ The pipeline is in your buyer's comments, not yours.

9. Someone lands on your profile and stays confused
↳ If they can't tell what you do in 5 seconds, they leave.

10. Your content tries to speak to everyone at once
↳ Written for the whole feed, so it lands with no one specific.

11. Every post teaches something but leads nowhere
↳ Great value, no direction, so the reader just scrolls on.

Tick more than 4 of these and your problem was never the content.

It's the system underneath it.

## B534 | 2026-07-15 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
You're reading the new LinkedIn algorithm wrong.

Posts that pulled 40,000 impressions last year do closer to 4,000 now.

Everyone reads that as the platform dying.

But it's the opposite.

LinkedIn is rebuilding who sees your work.

Three shifts driving it:

1. Your posts live for weeks, not hours
↳ A post used to die in 48 hours. Now LinkedIn keeps re-serving it for 2 to 3 weeks, as long as it holds attention.

2. Fewer people see it, and better people see it
↳ Your post goes to people who engaged with your topic in the last 30 days, viewed your profile, or talk to you often.

3. Your inbox now moves your reach
↳ Accounts that send and answer DMs get pushed further. Accounts that ignore the inbox drop to the second tier.

Here's the part you might not want to hear.

LinkedIn only changed because we did.

• Buyers stopped scrolling and started searching.
↳ So the algorithm got stricter about who it puts you in front of.

• Conversations moved into the DMs.
↳ So LinkedIn started reading your inbox as a signal.

• AI filled the feed with the same recycled post.
↳ So anything templated gets buried.

The algorithm is following our behaviour, nothing else.

We all say we don't care about vanity metrics.

The complaining says otherwise.

But that's the wrong fight.

More people are on LinkedIn than ever, so it has to get sharper about showing the right work to the right people.

Yes, you lost 40,000 impressions.

The ones who cared were never in that number.

Small reach to the right people beats big reach to no one.

♻️ Repost this if it reframed the algorithm for you.
💙 Follow me, Bhavya Goel, for how personal brands win on the new LinkedIn.

## B535 | 2026-07-16 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
I close more clients in the LinkedIn DMs than on any sales call.

No pitching, no chasing. A ten-step conversation that does the selling for me.

Here is the exact flow:

1. Source the lead
↳ Every lead comes from organic content. A lot is inbound, the rest are people who engage with my posts and I reach back out.

2. Pre-qualify
↳ Before I say a word, I check they fit my ICP. B2B founders posting on LinkedIn but not seeing the ROI yet.

3. Open warm
↳ I ask them to tell me about themselves and their business. Interest first, trust second, pitch never at this stage.

4. Map the now
↳ I find what is working, what isn't, and what they've tried. That context shapes everything after.

5. Map the goal
↳ I ask what their ideal outcome looks like. If they don't know where they are headed, I cannot take them there.

6. Find the block
↳ I dig into what is stopping them. People struggle to name it until you ask the right question. That gap is where the problem sits.

7. Weigh the pain
↳ I ask how long it has been going on. Long means high priority. New means they may not be ready to buy.

8. Test urgency
↳ I check if fixing this matters right now. If it can wait, they will struggle to commit.

9. Qualify the fit
↳ If they tick every box, I share the offer or suggest a call. On calls I let Granola take the notes so I stay fully present.

10. Close or learn
↳ Yes is a new client. No sends me hunting for the objection. Every conversation becomes a data point that feeds my next post.

The DM is not where you pitch.

It is where you listen until the offer becomes obvious.

## B537 | 2026-07-28 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
You type "make this better" into Claude, then

wonder why the post flops.

The problem was never Claude.

It was the instruction.

Feed it a vague ask and it fills the gaps with the most average version of the internet.

That is where the generic, everyone-sounds-the-same slop comes from.

Here are 7 prompt swaps that change what you get back, starting today:

1. "Make it more professional
↳ "Rewrite so my ICP [describe them] gets it in 5 seconds

2. "Improve it
↳ "Tighten it with 3 filters: clarity, rhythm, one clear CTA

3. "Make it sound natural
↳ "Rewrite the way I would say it out loud, no jargon. Ask me if you need context

4. "Give me 3 versions
↳ "Give me 3 versions with different hook angles, and tell me which you would publish and why

5. "Make the hook better
↳ "Give me 5 hook variations: one stat-led, one contrarian, one personal, one question-led, one story-led

6. "Give me content ideas
↳ "Give me 10 ideas a [founder type] would screenshot, based on these pains: [paste]

7. "Write the CTA
↳ "Write 3 CTAs: one for saves, one for comments, one for DMs. Tell me which fits this post

None of these are complicated. They are specific.

And specific is the whole difference between output that sounds like everyone else and output that sounds like you.

💾 Save this before your next prompt session.

♻️ Repost so your network gets better output too.

## B540 | 2026-07-31 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
One of my clients hit 138,566 impressions on a

single post.

She's an executive coach who works with senior leaders.

Here's what made it work:

1. The hook fit in two lines
↳ A question, then a one-word command. Short enough that LinkedIn never cut it off. The whole point was visible before anyone had to click.

2. The body was labeled like a framework, not written like an opinion
↳ Four named sections acting as a table of contents. Framework content gets saved. Opinion content gets liked once and forgotten.

3. The CTA was one question with nothing attached
No pitch, no link. One question, answerable in a sentence. Her ratio proves it: 102 comments against 343 reactions, when the average post runs a tenth of that.

This is what happens when every line in a post has a job to do.

Robyn remembers you.

Built in SF. Day one of seven.

I went through 100+ AI companies founded by women and headquartered in San Francisco, then sorted them by industry. One company a day for a week.

Consumer today.

## B553 | 2026-09-21 | linkedin | drafter: Bhavya
Your company that is building on models pays per token and that amount decides whether you can offer free tier to your customers or not.

#BuiltinSF. Day 2 out of 7.

OneTriangle sits underneath it all and makes your token usage cost less.

A small model handles the heavy context up front. Then it hands the cache over to a bigger model to finish the job.

You get the big model's answer. You skip the big model's compute on the long inputs.

It runs as a serving layer, so nothing changes inside your application.

Hannah Chung, co-founder and CEO and Medha Venkatapathy, co-founder and CTO built this and were able to provide measurable solutions to their customers on day one.

Their current benchmark: prefill cost across a million requests down to $7500 from $46,900.
